## DAY 18 -- late morning: Berra's little room (play)
- @@* (situation, no roll): Cari slept from about dawn to just before noon -- five hours or so, deep. What wakes her: a small cold weight on her cheek. The BOY, awake, propped on his mother, has leaned down off the bed and set the POTATO against her face (still on its scarf). Watching to see what she does. TESSA dozing upright. The dressing clean and dry; the hand pale, not shiny; the red lines faded to pink, short of mid-forearm now. Kitchen noise through the wall; Berra's voice at someone; a horse being led in the yard (Kade's mare -- the noon ride).
- Cari grins; pretends to GOBBLE the potato. Then "shh" -- let his mother sleep.
- @@* BOY (from situation -- a toddler, ~2, mended): a delighted squeak; claps his good hand over his own mouth, copying her shh; eyes huge over it. Takes the potato back and pretends to feed it to HIMSELF.
- Cari sits up; wipes drool and sleep-crust; out with the HAIRBRUSH (Merrit's rescue -- a prized possession); to work on her hair.
- Takes stock of her clothes. [Lee] the WHITE is second-hand; she probably shed it when the room got hot, but not before it took road grime, soot and blood. OLD SABETH (the laundress, S1/S11) will have a week of gleeful complaint. The BLUE was still on beneath -- likely a mess too. Idea: turn the White inside out.
- @@* (situation, no roll): the WHITE over the back of Berra's stool where she shed it before the lancing: road mud dried grey to the knee; soot down one sleeve (Berra's kitchen hearth); at the right cuff a brown stiff patch of the boy's blood and pus, soaked through. The BLUE (thin silk) slept-in and creased to rags; a honey smear and a greasy thumb of Hannet's salve down the front; it smells of horse. The WHITE SASH still tied; the YELLOW slipped half out. Inside-out, the White is a clean, plain, seam-ribbed robe -- except the cuff, where the stain came through.

## DAY 18 -- noon: Berra's kitchen, the yard door (Kade leaves)
- Present: Cari; KADE (riding clothes, the staff -- Fax 5); HOLLIS; BERRA (at the board); DUNNY (scouring the porridge pot). The BOY and TESSA through the doorway. ASHER (rushlight).

- GM DECISION @@* (situation, set now): HOLM unthreaded the KEY from the padlock once the cell was open and counted it into his book with the coin -- "one key, iron, the brother's own" (iron is rare and sacred; Holm would not leave it lying). It's in HOLM's counting-chest (NOT the press box with the leaves). KADE was on the stair and saw it.
- WKW: KADE knows Cari can appear through her lantern and keeps a fire spirit (S14). He does NOT know the Scry Eye rides on the key. He can guess "a working."
- @@* KADE (from character -- few words, a decent man doing a hard sum, no dialect): "Holm has it. Counted in with the coin." A look at her, not asking. "I'll get it off him." Then, the practical question a messenger needs: "And what do I tell the Prior -- what day?"
- Cari: "I hope to leave before I sleep, but if not I'll be on the road in the morning unless some emergency pops up. Tell him I will keep my mouth shut." (grin) Points at the staff: "Want me to douse that?"
- WKW CHECK: KADE does NOT know the decoded message or the Prior's order about the name -- "keep my mouth shut" means nothing to him. He carries it word for word, unasked.
- TIMING (situation, from record): a rider is faster than the coach on the flat; the switchbacks are walking pace either way. Leaving at noon, Kade reaches the switchbacks late afternoon and the gate about DUSK -- the third bend near dark.
- @@* KADE (from character): "Word for word." (No question about it.) On the light: a glance at the staff-head, then at the sky. "Leave it. I'll be on the switchbacks at dusk." A beat. "Can you put it out from here? After."
- (Rules: a caster can always let go of her own Persistent spell -- dismissing, register sec 11. Distance: the Fax isn't anchored to her by a line; @@ whether she can dismiss at range is Lee's/Cari's call -- leave it to her answer.)
- Cari: "I can, but I won't know when it's good. You might get held up. It can wait till morning." -> KADE's STAFF Fax stays through the night; Cari dismisses it at/after the Day 19 dawn (or the Dawn takes it).
- [Lee] RULING (dismissing at range): she CAN let go of a distant casting. If she can't SEE it, the GM may require a roll -- on a fail she puts out the WRONG one; on an Extreme failure she might even lose the BINDING. "That's something that is more dependent on the dramatic impact. If it's fun, roll it; if it's boring, don't bother." -> gap log / register sec 11 (Dismissing).
- @@* KADE: a nod -- that suits him. Mounts; the staff across the saddle-bow. Whistles once, low. HOLLIS peels himself off her shins with visible reluctance, a last shove of the head into her hand, and goes. Kade looks back once from the yard gate; lifts the staff an inch (the little flame); rides north at a walk, then a trot.
- WKW: KADE rides for the Mountain at noon, Day 18, with the Prior's report and her message: up before she sleeps if she can, else on the road in the morning barring emergency; "I will keep my mouth shut."
- Scene ends.

## DAY 18 -- afternoon: Berra's kitchen (the balm)
- Present: Cari; BERRA; TESSA (Robbie fed, asleep with his potato); DUNNY. ASHER (rushlight / Berra's oven).
- Tokens 5 (new scene; at cap). Hook budget 4.
- PRECEDENT (S06 Pt2 l.284): HAND SALVE (goose fat + beeswax + crushed juniper): Apothecary vs 2d -> effect 2 = a +2 Boost to healing chapped hands. Medicine is Gear: a Persistent Wager / a pool of Boost given in doses (register sec 9). MAKING = Apothecary (EL12); TREATING = Healer.
- NOTE for later (register sec 8): an old woman's frost-swollen knuckles = most likely CHRONIC -> a permanent Hindrance on health rolls; a balm EASES (Boost), it does not cure. Diagnosis (See the Root / a look) not yet made.
- @@* BERRA's LARDER (situation; what a Crossroads inn kitchen in winter has): a crock of good LARD (yes, she'll spare it -- "for Aldy, take the crock"); tallow CANDLE-ENDS; a little BEESWAX (a cake, kept for her bread-cloths @@*); MUSTARD SEED (ground, for the mutton); dried ROSEMARY and THYME; GARLIC; ONIONS; SALT; VINEGAR; a small dear twist of black PEPPER (southern, she guards it). No juniper.
- CARI's SATCHEL (after Robbie; standing kit, depleted): some WILLOW BARK; COMFREY and YARROW (a little); FEVERFEW and ELDER; a little BRANDY; SALT; the rest of HANNET's salve (a thumb's worth). Honey -> Tessa.
- @@* BERRA: "Lard's for Aldy, take the crock." Sets out what she has without being asked twice; the pepper last, and she watches where it goes. TESSA: wipes her hands on her skirt, ready. "What do I do?"
- [Lee] RECIPE: whatever her training says might work -- "a little lard and the beeswax with an aromatic, maybe the MUSTARD, to stimulate circulation. Cari would know better." BERRA must SEE the construction -- "this end is just a recipe." Put together and blended warm into a small crock. Roll it first: "put the Boost into the jar."
- @@* CARI's RECIPE (her training; plain folk remedy): lard and the beeswax melted together in a small crock set in a pan of hot water (never over the flame -- it scorches); crushed ROSEMARY and the last of the COMFREY steeped in it at a bare simmer the better part of an hour; strained through boiled linen; as it cools, a PINCH of ground MUSTARD and a grind of Berra's PEPPER stirred in -- "too much and it blisters thin old skin"; tested on her own wrist; poured into the crock to set.
- WKW: BERRA watched the whole making (and TESSA) -- they have the recipe.
- RULES READ: assist-rule.md (things with their own capabilities Boost, capped by the user's Role rank); register sec 9 (medicine is Gear; a batch's effect is a POOL of Boost doled out in doses, a dose capped at the administering hand's relevant rank -- "guidance kept"); precedent: S06 Pt2 hand salve, Apothecary vs 2d, effect = Boost.
- ROLL 1 (TESSA's help -- a Boost action: she grinds the mustard and minds the water-bath): base Level 2 @@* (young townswoman) vs DL1 (routine).
[Tessa helps (Boost, Lv2)] 2d: 0 0 = 0
[DL1 routine] 1d: 1 = 1
- RESULT: 0 vs 1 -> LOSS: no Boost (2 dice: no Extreme). Fiction @@*: three nights awake -- she lets the water-bath boil dry for a moment and the first melt catches; Cari tips it out and starts again. Nothing lost but a little lard and ten minutes.
- ROLL 2: Cari APOTHECARY EL12 vs the balm's difficulty 2d (precedent; mustard is touchy, no juniper).
[Cari Apothecary: Aldy's balm (EL12)] 12d: 1 0 0 1 0 1 0 0 0 0 1 0 = 4
[the balm resists (mustard, wax, no juniper)] 2d: 0 0 = 0
- RESULT: 4 vs 0 -> EFFECT 4. (Opposition all-blank on 2 dice: no Extreme.) GEAR: ALDY's BALM -- a small crock, a POOL of 4 Boost against stiff, swollen, frost-bitten joints (warming; eases the morning stiffness). Sets soft and green-gold; smells of rosemary with a bite under it.
- @@ OPEN for Lee: the dose cap is "the administering hand's relevant rank." Cari (Healer 4) can give the whole pool; ALDY rubbing it in herself has no Healer rank -- does she get nothing (assist-rule.md reading), or does a balm, being plain Gear with no capabilities of its own, sit outside the Assist Rule? -> gap log if he rules.
- [Lee] RULING (applying medicine vs making it): a skill that needs a Role (making a medicine from what's at hand) is different from something ANYONE can do (rubbing a liniment or balm onto someone). Just APPLYING it should generally allow BASE LEVEL -- "which is something." Not the Assist Rule: "It's not so much Assist as just a simple Boost." Lee's one concern: the Boost may give someone enough dice to allow a CRIT -- "as likely bad as good." -> gap log; register sec 9 (Medicine), beside the dose cap.
- Cari takes a dollop and TESSA's hands and APPLIES it, teaching as she goes: massage it in; rub the muscles at the base of the THUMB PAD; show her (gently!) why you DON'T squeeze the knuckles -- just rub, "like milking a very small and prissy goat." Massaging up the arms is good ("See? Isn't that nice?") but don't waste the balm. Any old grease would do at need; the herbs and the wax are refinements -- the point is to get the joints WARM and LOOSE enough not to hurt so much. Then TESSA does the same back to Cari. Teaching her this one technique -- the important bits that make her base Level apply.
- NO ROLL (teaching a willing learner, nothing resists; Lee's intent: Tessa now applies the balm at her base Level).
- @@* TESSA (from character): self-conscious at first, then absorbed; she laughs out loud at the goat. Her own healing kettle-blister tingles under the mustard and she says so ("It's warm. It's WARM."). Her second pass on Cari's hands is slow and careful and right; she remembers the thumb pad without being told.
- WKW: TESSA knows how to apply the balm (warm, loose, rub don't squeeze, the thumb pad, up the arm). BERRA watched this too.
- Cari picks up ROBBIE and takes TESSA (and the crock) to visit MOTHER ALDY. [Lee] "THIS part is what I needed her for."
- CHECKED (S13 l.928-1380): ALDY -- widow of CORRAN the THATCHER (died last winter); Cari laid him to rest (Day 11/12 @@); bad KNEES (didn't get up); sly, cackles, "Go on, girl"; teaching TOM FENN the thatcher's knots with Corran's tools (leggett, needles, shearing hook) in her shed; held the ladder forty years; pats cheeks like a grandmother's; the pump-gossip ("Wat's to go up for Liss Fenn's roof"). She has met Cari; knows her as "the Sister" / "girl."

[Tessa applies the balm (Lv2 + dose 2)] 4d: 1 1 1 1 = 4 (EXTREME SUCCESS)
[Aldy's stiffness (Complication 2)] 2d: 0 1 = 1

## DAY 18 -- mid-afternoon: the TANNERS' END, BERTIL's house

Wynn is about her time (days, maybe a week). The first should come straight; the SECOND will need TURNING or bringing by the feet once the first is out -- skilled hands, quickly. Risk after: BLEEDING (her history; a stretched womb bleeds more). Wynn herself: tired, pale at the lips and nails (thin blood @@ -- she hasn't been eating for one, let alone three), swollen ankles; no fever; strong heart. Nothing urgent TODAY.

Effect vs Level 3 (core: effect = Level is a good read with the finest detail held back; over Level is complete): under 3 -- "a big baby, lying oddly"; 3 -- something lies across up top, wrong for a single child; over 3 -- TWINS, the lie of each, and that the second will need turning.

Two small flames: the LOWER one head-down, settled, strong and steady (the heartbeat she heard); the UPPER one smaller, curled CROSSWISE under the right ribs like a sleeping cat, its back to the light, its own heartbeat quick and faint under its twin's. ## DAY 18 -- late afternoon: the FENN COTTAGE
- Present: Cari; LISS; TOM (8); MOLL; LINNET (baby); HARROW; WAT PRYCE (on the roof). ASHER (free by day; the Fenn hearth carries her Fax 8).

A ladder up the gable. WAT PRYCE on it (council-man Pryce's son @@ -- S13 named the council's roll-keeper PRYCE), sleeves up, a leggett in his fist, finishing the last course before the light goes. TOM on the ground handing up bundles, as promised -- "I can cut, and bundle, and carry, and hand it up." HARROW on an upturned bucket by the door in Rook's da's drover coat, telling them both they're doing it wrong. ## DAY 19 -- morning: the ROAD NORTH (forest road), Asher's rounds
- Present: Cari (on the grey); ASHER (bond; away on his rounds). Nobody on the road.
- Cari, mindspeech to ASHER: "I have made my rounds as best I could. It's your turn. Don't forget Old Bellows and the House. Please give them my best and let them know I miss them, but duties demand my attendance elsewhere."
- CHECKED (S09-S14 briefs): Asher's ROUNDS = Mirna's lodge fire, COBB's hearth (Saltwind; Fax 3, anchor line; THIMBLE keeps the skeps under an Agreement, Cari surety), MIRA's coals (MIN), the Court (the WEEPING COURT HOUSE -- "needs someone to take care of"; her leaf a guest-gift: "not a debt, a door"). OLD BELLOWS in Garrow's forge (S13: "Bring the bossy one back. I want to hear about his OVEN" -- the bossy one = ASHER, who kept Berra's oven).

- GM TRUTH (set before the roll): through the stone, the great hearth holds OLD SOOT -- an old, hunched, banked shape in the deep coals at the back, dark red, slow, like a great toad of embers with eyes half-lidded; he notices the stone and looks BACK. With the Cold Down, his ROOTS run down through the hearthstone into the Mountain, and up them, very faint and very slow, comes WARMTH from far below, pulsing like a sleeper's breath (the Sleeper stirred, S14). Opposition: the Borderlands' veil on an old deep spirit, Level 1 (he isn't hiding). Under 1: an old face in the fire, maybe; 1: OLD SOOT, a shape, old, looking back at him; over 1: plus the roots going down, and the slow warm pulse coming up.
[Jem through the hag-stone (base Lv1)] 1d: 1 = 1
[Old Soot, deep and old (Lv1)] 1d: 0 = 0
- RESULT: 1 vs 0 -> EFFECT 1 = Level 1: a good read, the finest detail held back -- JEM sees OLD SOOT: the old banked shape in the back coals, half-lidded, and it LOOKS BACK at him. Not the roots; not the warmth below.

- GM DECISION @@* (the letter's CONTENTS, broad strokes -- written by Pryce ~Day 15, BEFORE Robbie's night; plain Common in a careful upright hand, one stiff Celestial greeting copied out letter by letter):
  1. GREETING to Aldric, Prior, from the Council of Crossroads (Pryce, roll-keeper, for the council).
  2. THANKS: the ring put back "into Ebba Oxley's own hand"; his letter read in the square and pegged by the shrines "where all may read it"; the Sister's day of healing "for no coin."
  3. NOTICE (respectful, not defiant): the council and muster have agreed that when any cart of the house comes, the muster will WALK IT IN, spears grounded, "as witness to what it brings, that no one may say after it took." And: "No cart came this autumn. We note it. We do not complain of it."
  4. THE REQUEST: a circuit priest, "proper," on a set round -- none since the summer; a year of the sick untended, the dead without rites (Corran the thatcher lay a year without the words), a boy's leg set late "for want of silver first" -- "we would not have that again."
  5. AN OFFER: the town will keep the circuit priest's bed and board at the inn ("Mes Berra has agreed," underlined) and a share of the shrine bowls for the road.
  6. A careful line: "We do not presume to name whom the house should send." Then, below it, added in the same hand but smaller, as if after argument: "We would not refuse the Sister."
  7. SIGNED: Pryce for the Council; BRAM OXLEY's name (muster); GARROW HASK's name; five or six marks (Wat Pryce's among them @@*); witnessed "at the well."
- @@* PRIOR (from character): turns the letter over before opening it; looks at the seal a long moment -- the cross-in-ring, the thumbprint pressed into the edge. "Your doing?" (the wax). Reads it once fast, then again slowly, Odo waiting. At line 6 the corner of his mouth moves. Sets it down square on the desk, one finger on it. "It's a good letter." Then: "'They will walk it in.'" Quiet. "Good. Let them." A beat, and the dry look: "'We would not refuse the Sister.'" He lets that lie on the desk between them. "That line will be read at Convocation, Initiate. By someone. Whether I read it or not."
- Cari, warmly flattered: "He asked me to read it first. I thought it better not to. I had no idea what might be in it."

## DAY 20 -- None: the PRECENTOR's STUDY, the VISITOR and the leaves
- Present: Cari; the PRIOR; ODO; GODRIC; IVRO (chair); HOLM; MERRIT (asleep). ASHER (bond).

## DAY 20 -- late afternoon into supper: MAGS's KITCHEN (Jem's first letters)
- Present: Cari; JEM; NELL; MAGS; scullions; OLD SOOT. ASHER (bond).

- Cari (directed primarily to the PRIOR -- she doesn't know how much he's told the Visitor; doesn't want to open too many layers at once): "Should I assume the Father Visitor has already read the reports of Clerk Fennick, Finder Kade, Infirmarer Hannet, and Stablemaster Olwin, and the signed statements of Clerk Odo, Precentor Ivro and the Prior, so that I may assume the Father already has ALL the necessary context?"
- GM DECISION @@* (what GODRIC has been given -- from the Prior's character: exact, protective, keeps his own orders; wrote S12 that the case is beyond him):
  - GIVEN: the Prior's letter (S12); ODO's BOOK entire (the hearings, OLWIN's sworn words before the chapter, the steward's knife, Sever and Brask taken, Tobin's drawer and the B. tally-book entries AS LISTED, Pellam's confession, the sulphur); HANNET's infirmary book (Calder's hands, ungloved; Merrit; Teague's cold-burns); IVRO's witnessed reading of the scraped charter; the CUSTOMARY and its cancel; the leaves' faces. And last night the PRIOR took GODRIC down to the UNDERCROFT -- the table, the socket, the chalk, the marks (the Visitor must judge Calder's "Watch").
  - NOT GIVEN: FENNICK's copies (with Fennick, in Ganth); a written report from KADE (he reported to the Prior by mouth; his written reports went to Brask); OLWIN's report beyond what he swore before the chapter; the CIPHER, the decoded message, the NAME; Cari's dream.
- @@* GODRIC (from character -- dry, before the Prior can answer): "I have read what this house wrote down, Initiate." A beat. "I have not read what it didn't." The PRIOR (from character, exact): "The Visitor has my letter, Odo's book entire, Hannet's, the Precentor's reading, the Customary. Last night I showed him the room beneath the chapter house." A beat. "Not Fennick's copies -- they're in Ganth. The Finder reported to me by mouth. Olwin's words are as he swore them." (Nothing of the cipher -- by his silence, she understands.) GODRIC: "So I have the CONTEXT." The millstone, patient, unmoved: "What I don't have is YOUR eyes. I asked what you saw."
- WKW: CARI -- what Godric has (above); the Prior showed him the UNDERCROFT last night.
- Cari: apologises -- not meaning to be obtuse; doesn't wish to waste the Visitor's time. If he hasn't read Fennick's account or heard Kade's, he may not know of their INITIAL BATTLE with the Hierarch (she'll keep his title -- not stripped yet) through the conduit of his NECROMANCY, PUPPETING the corpse of their beloved late Hospitaller, BROTHER AUBRIC, whom she thereafter laid to rest. The first time they crossed and SAW each other through that channel, each knowing for certain who the adversary was. The Hierarch had already been wearing GLOVES at Table for months; that was when she knew him beyond doubt, and INJURED him so that others began to notice how he held them. "Does such vision count, or should I limit the tale to what physical eyes can corroborate?"
- WKW: GODRIC, the PRIOR, ODO (writing) -- Cari's charge, in her words: Calder used NECROMANCY to puppet Aubric's corpse; she fought him through it; saw him; injured him; the gloves were on before that; she laid Aubric to rest. (The Prior knew much of this -- S11 Ivro's account; Odo's book has Wystan's "Aubric lies at Saltwind, laid there by the Initiate's hand.")
- CHECKED (S11 l.113): CALDER's own story at the high table: at the coast he laid hands on a fiend's "witch-fire" to put down a DEAD BROTHER the fiend had raised; burned to the wrists. -- the MIRROR of hers.
- @@* GODRIC (from character): at "Necromancy" the millstone stops. A long silence. Then, low and very even: "That is a word for a court of the Order, Initiate, not a hearing in a Prior's room. Say it in chapter and you will be asked to stand behind it with more than your eyes." A beat. "But you've said it here. ODO -- write it as she said it." ODO writes. Then: "The Sight counts. This Order has always taken it, and always weighed it -- as it weighs eyes." And the turn: "The Hierarch tells me this story too. The same night, I'd wager -- the coast, a dead brother walking, a fire. In his telling the FIRE was a fiend's, and the dead brother the fiend's puppet, and HE put it down and burned his hands doing it." He spreads his own big hands on his knees. "So I have two priests of the Order, one dead brother, and a fire. Each of you says the other was the hand on the corpse." The millstone: "Tell me what you saw. Then tell me who else saw it."
- [Lee] Cari tells the tale as she gave it to the Prior before, in succinct detail, like a clerk's report; thorough within relevance (he's made it clear they're not here to hurry). No point adding the CALLER unless a detail draws it in. Hides nothing, including her own BRIMSTONE moments where relevant. "Hiding or omitting what someone else has already reported is worse."
- CHECKED (S04 ledger, Tanner's Ford, night -- the record): the ghola in the reeds ~40 paces east of the ford; her Second Sight found it (brimstone finds brimstone -- it TASTED the demon trace in her, Hook); a LEASH of dull greasy gold running NORTH; her Guidable Fax sent out over the water -> plain-eye reveal to the camp (drovers shouted; Hollis bayed): a gaunt grey man, filmed eyes, a robe gone mud-coloured, a healer's satchel strap, a ring of small brass KEYS (one iron with an ORANGE ribbon Aubric tied there); AUBRIC surfaced: "...Cari." / "Don't... look at me, little sister." / "He walks me... to the sea. To the woman... who shamed him." (MIRA) -- KADE HEARD it speak her name, lowered his chain; her REBUKE down the leash with the silver hairpin ("How DARE you") -- the leash SNAPPED, burned out end to end, and 2 excess burned back into the HOLDER's HANDS (burned palms, headache, knocked from his chair); in that instant she GLIMPSED the far end: candlelight, a TATTOOED SCALP, a light contralto scream -- CALDER; the holder TASTED THE DEMON in her (Hook; Purification evidence); Aubric GAVE his TRUE NAME freely and asked to be let go; her EXORCISM by the True Name -- his shade went on, the taint burned in her salt ring sulphur-yellow, a SEAL on his rest; his last words: "There. Fire taught you something worth knowing... Well done, little sister." KADE closed the eyes and said the office for the dead; took the body to SALTWIND for burial under the right name; she said the words. The KEYS: Kade put them in her pocket. And after (S4 bath): she confessed to her gods the PLEASURE she took in Calder's pain, and prayed not to become what she despised.
- CARI's REPORT (as given -- GM summary of her clerk's account, from the record above; Lee's direction): place, night, company (Korrin's train; Kade and Hollis; the drovers; Jem sick in the wagon); the Sight-find and the brimstone-kinship (said plainly: "it knew me by the same mark the Hierarch has since spoken of"); the light; what the camp SAW by plain eye; Aubric's words and who heard them (Kade); the leash by Sight; her rebuke; the line burning back; the glimpse of the far end (Sight); that the holder TASTED what is in her (said plainly -- this is where the Hierarch learned it); the True Name freely given; the exorcism and the seal; the burial at Saltwind; the keys; and, briefly, the pleasure she took in his pain, confessed to her gods since. ODO writes all of it.
- WITNESSES (for "who else saw"): by PLAIN EYE -- KADE (the ghola, the keys, Aubric speaking her name, the exorcism, the body), KORRIN, WICK and the DROVERS (the dead man in the light), HOLLIS; the body and grave -- SALTWIND (the chapel yard; Wystan's word in chapter); the KEYS (@@ where now -- returned to the house?). By SIGHT only -- herself; ASHER (her bound spirit -- a spirit's testimony @@). Jem was sick in the wagon (saw little @@).
- NO ROLL (testimony; nothing contested in the room -- the contest is in the Visitor's judgment, later).
- @@* GODRIC (from character -- exact; listens without a sound to the end; ODO's pen the only noise): when she finishes, a long silence. "The Finder is in this house." Not a question. To the PRIOR: "I'll hear him next. Before Brother Pellam." Then to Cari, one question, quietly, the judge's question: "When did the Hierarch FIRST say you were demon-blooded, Initiate? Before that night at the ford -- or after?"
- GM-ONLY: the logic Godric is testing -- if Calder named her demon-touched only AFTER the ford, the knowledge came down HIS OWN LEASH (proof his hand was on the corpse). Record: the PURIFICATION LIST ticked 2/4 -> 3/4 at the ford "direct evidence of demon-taint, tasted by Calder himself"; before that, the laundry's sulphur talk (S1, her robes named to the Hierarch's clerks -- 2/4) and Calder's protest at her ordination to the RED (S08 canon; when? -- before S1 @@). So: the CLERKS' sulphur question came BEFORE (S1); the TASTE, AFTER. What CARI knows of the timeline: she knew of the clerks' question (Mags, S1: "You leave the laundry alone"); she knew Calder stormed out of her Red ordination. Her answer is Lee's.
- Cari wipes the tears of retelling Aubric's end; composes herself. It was AUBRIC's insistence that she take the RED, and she believes he meant it as a good-natured jibe at the Hierarch; she'd always assumed Calder stormed out because she passed the test on a TECHNICALITY (a spell, not a spirit). She doesn't know whether his vendetta began before or after that, but for some time he'd been ASKING QUESTIONS and had even CONFISCATED her Red and other garments from the laundry; MES SABETH answered him, and it burdens her that she answered truthfully that yes, the things smell of brimstone. Then, meeting his eyes calmly: "The first time I have heard the word DEMON applied to me since my father sold me to Precentor Ivro at nine years old was this moment, from your own lips. The Precentor has BEEN my father by all lights I care about ever since, and a good one, and I have earned four OTHER Colors without a 'technicality' in service of our god, by the Tenets and the Tolls." She doesn't raise her voice; she wants the moment crystallised.
- CHECKED (S13 l.227-252): her only formal charge (Brask's) was MALEFICIUM on Olwin -- answered, "I find it nothing." No "demon" charge on record. Her claim stands.

(The PRIOR knew the Ivro purchase? @@ -- WKW unclear; ODO now has it in the book.)
- @@* GODRIC (from character -- exact; fair; takes a correction when it's due): holds her eyes. Then, without looking away, to the desk: "ODO. Write that the word 'demon' was the HIERARCH's -- said to me in his room yesterday -- and that I repeated it here; and that this is the first the Initiate has heard it said of her in this house." The pen. Then, to her, the millstone, and something under it: "You're right to want that written." A beat. "And the Colours are in the book. I counted them." (He did.) A breath; to the Prior: "That will do for this morning. Send for the Finder."

- @@* (situation): at the door she passes KADE coming in, Ruddock behind him, HOLLIS left sitting in the passage with an air of grievance. Kade's eyes meet hers for one beat. Nothing said. He goes in. The door shuts.

## DAY 21 -- late morning: the passage outside the PRIOR's room

POCKETS (the Orange): the lead token in Ruddock's kerchief (cold, asleep); the mirror in Blue silk; Rook's rubbing; and @@* a heel of breakfast TOAST she pocketed at Mags's table without thinking (Aubric's rule: feed whoever comes hungry -- habit).

Not done -- it'll take the day.

To the GATE by way of the WELL, with a EWER and a TRAY OF CUPS, so everyone gets a drink.

The GOODS: ordinary -- grain, beans, wool, a few sacks with the faint greasy residue of fear and wanting that handled money leaves; PELLAM's small locked CHEST: his FEAR thick on it like grease, and old greed -- nothing cold, nothing alive. And UNDERFOOT -- very faint, so faint she might be imagining it -- the frozen yard is not cold all the way down: under the stone, far below, something like the warmth of a great sleeping flank, rising and falling, slow as a season. (The SLEEPER stirred while the Cold is Down -- S14 "below turned... warm." She has felt the sigh before.)
- WKW: CARI -- the goods are clean; Pellam's chest holds only his fear and greed; the warmth under the yard.

Very low, the honest bit: "Since Ma, she's the first one who wanted me that I didn't have to be useful to first." (Hook: Ma Always Said She'd Take Me -- careful.) A beat. "...You're the second."

A hug: she wants him here, but that's selfish; nothing here for him now that won't be here later, unless she isn't. "NOT TO WORRY" -- don't worry about what you can't help; everything is as fine as it's going to be. A few seasons a drover and Korrin will take good care of him and he may decide that's what he wants; stay now and she'll work with him now, and that might be all his passions allow. Waits to see whether PIP wants the rinse or the mangle; takes whichever's left. Then: she could get a sturdy lad who's spending all his time in the kitchen to come help too, if Sabeth promises not to steal him from NELL -- "oh, DEAR GODS, THEY ARE SO CUTE TOGETHER. - WKW: SABETH, PIP -- the Sister thinks Jem and Nell are sweet on each other (and means to meddle).

## DAY 21 -- late afternoon: the YARD (Korrin)

## DAY 21 -- evening, Vespers to Compline: MAGS's KITCHEN (scouring)
- Present: Cari; MAGS; two scullions; OLD SOOT. (JEM and NELL at the laundry, folding.) ASHER (bond).
- Tokens 5. Hook budget 4.
- NO ROLLS (drudge work).
- @@* KITCHEN GOSSIP (Mags's and the scullions'; situation -- what a kitchen hears):
  - The FINDER came out of the Visitor's room and ate two bowls and said nothing -- "which in him means it went well."

[  vs age 1] 1d: 0 = 0

[  vs age 7] 7d: 1 0 1 0 0 1 1 = 4
[DAWN 22 IVRO EL5] 5d: 1 0 1 1 0 = 3

- RESULTS: CALDER-HEARTH 3 vs 0, EXTREME SUCCESS on its own roll AGAIN -> holds at 3, AGE RESETS to 1 (second dawn running). FENN HEARTH 5 vs 3 -> HOLDS at 6 (age 12). BEND 4 vs 4 TIE -> HOLDS at 8 (age 8). LAVABO #3 3 vs 0 -> HOLDS at 6 (age 2; age die 1 -- no Extreme). SCRY EYE 1 vs 4 -> LOSS by 3 = strength 3 -> GONE (the Eye on Pellam's key, in Kade's pouch, ends). IVRO 3 vs 2 -> WIN, no harm. THE COLD 3 vs 4 -> no recovery (CLOSE -- by one): HARM 9, DOWN; the token sleeps.
- GM NOTE: the Cold's recovery came within a single success. Its odds stay poor (4d vs 9d) but a good dawn could do it. When it recovers: the lean returns to the fires; the HIERARCH's TOKEN's line WAKES (Calder would feel it moved, and where).
- OOC [Lee]: "You're giving it an extra die a day, yes?" -- No complaints (plot-relevant), but normally a recovery roll wouldn't get that. A PC might be in a coma a long time; a spirit effectively banished much the same way, for years, maybe centuries. PCs generally get Healers' Boosts, and can spend Tokens on those rolls (unless ruled otherwise); spirits get similar aid from a Conjuror summoning them back, or from the GM spending a Token. "Just mentioning it to be fair."
- GM ANSWER / CORRECTION: NO extra die -- every dawn since Day 16 has been the Cold's Level 4 (4d) vs its Harm 9 (9d), unchanged. Today's 3 vs 4 was plain luck. And the GM OVERSTATED the danger: per recovery.md / feeling-down.md, a WIN reduces Harm BY THE EFFECT (usually 1) -- it does NOT end Down; the Cold stays Down until its Harm drops BELOW its Level (Harm 3 or less) -- about six points of recovery. Odds of a 4d-vs-9d win on any dawn: ~4.6% (computed). So, unaided, it is Down for a LONG time -- Lee's coma/banishment reading is right. What could change that: AID (someone summoning or feeding it; a GM Token on its roll; Calder's own working, if he could reach it). STRIKE the "only by one / no longer remote" OOC note at Day 22 dawn.
- -> GAP LOG / register sec 8: spirit and coma recovery when Harm far exceeds Level -- the slow road; Boosts and Tokens are the levers.
- [Lee] Gets up; clean linens; a plain BROWN robe for now; makes sure the BLUE SASH is clean before she puts it on. (Aside: she needs to sit down sometime and make nice SOAPS and scented OILS -- when she gets a chance.) Out through the kitchens to make sure the drovers have BREAKFAST before they go, if she has to cook and carry something herself.

A voice like a dry reed. "You were heard by the Visitor yesterday. And today you're turning ticks in brown." Not a question yet. Then it is: "Is it penance? Or is it to be SEEN turning ticks?"
- Cari (a sigh -- she's been waiting for this; serious consideration): honestly, knowing it sounds bad, she doesn't recall offhand the last time she did a PENANCE; if she has offended him, she will do one now. As for being SEEN: she'd much prefer not to be, but she's likely to be called back to the Prior's chambers for the Visitor, so she must be easy to find -- hence the Blue; her hair makes her hard to miss either way, and hiding it is HIDING, which causes its own suspicions. She works; this is how she serves and when she says her prayers; she gets to services at least once a day when she can, but duty intervenes; an Initiate Priestess, she can say her OWN Vespers and Compline, which she often does. She won't tell another how they must judge her -- he may and MUST do as his conscience dictates; she doesn't fault him. (Shifts the ticking.) "Is there something I can do for you, Brother? Or did you merely need to hear the act from my own lips?" A rueful smile -- he'll hear what he chooses.

"Then you are very good, or very proud. Or very busy." A pause in which he seems honestly to weigh which. "I have been all three." Then, of the hair and the Blue, flat: "Hiding is hiding. Yes." (Agreement -- he says it as if it costs nothing, which for him it doesn't.) And the answer to her question, exact: "Yes. There's something you can do." A nod at his own cell, two doors down. "Leave my bed. I turn my own tick." The faintest thing at the corner of the mouth, gone at once. "Thirty years. I'm not about to be done for now." He goes on toward the kitchen with his dry bread.

Drops the ticking (unwieldy). If he feels her time's better spent in the infirmary, the library, the chapel, anywhere, he's welcome to direct her; she'll hear his advice with the respect due his age and likely take it if it fits the Tenets, the Tolls, and the CUSTOMARY ("which I HAVE read, cover to cover, and recently; apologies if that's pride again"). The test is not whether a priest carries a demon's mark -- the Order's priests are demon-handlers by the Tenets -- but whether the mark rules HIM. (Her brimstone flaring in anger before a prisoner -- Odo's book -- is exactly the "is ordered" argument.)

- RULES READ (wagered-actions.md; actions-and-timing.md "Boosts With Duration"): wagered points come off the EL BEFORE the roll; if the roll succeeds WITHOUT them, they become a NAMED bonus added to the EL of other relevant rolls; such Boosts "generally last the rest of the scene, though the GM may rule that it ... lasts longer, as appropriate." - GM TRUTH (set BEFORE the roll; @@* setting, Lee to true -- what's in the Rule that she didn't remember):
  (1) THREE TO STAND, a rider: "NONE WHO SITS MAY STAND." Whoever sits in judgment with the Convocation can't be one of the three who stand for the named -- so if the PRIOR sits, he can't stand for her.
  (2) EXAMINATION, a rider: the examined may ask that "THE SPIRIT BE ASKED" -- a spirit bound to the examined may be called to speak to whether he ORDERS or IS ORDERED. (ASHER.)
  (3) FALSE CHARGES, a rider: a charge must be laid "IN HIS OWN VOICE, before the Convocation, by name" -- a charge carried by letter, clerk or another's mouth is NO CHARGE (and the penalty clause bites only on a charge so laid).
  (4) FINEST DETAIL -- SOULS AS GOODS, a qualifier: "...SAVE IN WARDSHIP, AS A FATHER HIS CHILD, UNTO THEIR AGE." And beside it, in the gutter, IVRO's small square hand, the ink browner than his recent glosses (about ten years old): "What age? The Rule does not say. -- I." (He looked it up the year he bought her. The clause is a defence AND a hook: no age is given, and a PRICE PAID looks like goods.)
  TIERS (effect vs Level 3): under 3 -- (1) only; 3 -- (1) and (2), with references; over 3 -- all four, with exact article and leaf references, and Ivro's gloss.
- [Lee] CORRECTION (rules reading): "Wagers only ever actually retain the wagered value. No matter how well she succeeds, it's pass/fail unless she gets a crit." -> the PLAIN EFFECT does NOT tier anything. RETIRE the tiers above. A PASS (succeeds without the wager) = the named Boost at the wagered value AND what she went looking for -- all four riders (1)-(4), with references. A FAIL = no Boost, and the buried riders stay unfound (she knows only what she already knew).
- [Lee] WAGER 3 (Loremaster cap). EL9 - 3 = 6. EXTRA EFFORT +2 dice (dice.md): roll 8d; a TOTAL over 6 = complete failure (zero effect); no Extreme SUCCESS possible (pool != EL); Extreme failure still possible (all zero). Vs the Rule, Level 3 (3d).
[Cari Loremaster (Dogma): Rule notes, EL6 after wager 3, +2 extra effort (fail if total > 6)] 8d: 0 1 0 0 0 1 0 0 = 2
[  the Rule's archaism (Lv3)] 3d: 1 0 0 = 1
- RESULT: 2 vs 1 -> PASS (total 2, under the cap of 6). NAMED PERSISTENT BOOST: "RULE NOTES" +3 -- lasts through the CONVOCATION, on any roll where citing the Rule's actual text helps. Tokens 5 (unspent).

- RULES READ (roles/conjuror.md "Contracts"): a FORMAL Contract = the spirit agrees to TERMS with an OATH BY ITS TRUE NAME (Cari has it: True Name of Asher 3). It creates a CONTRACT HOOK on the spirit, REDUCED each time a service is rendered UNLESS some other payment is offered and accepted for that service. At the last service the spirit may CLOSE the Contract (must completely leave the mortal realm); if it doesn't, that is an implicit agreement to another service (the last rank reinstated). Hook + True Name = call it into attendance as a free action. "Many spirits will willingly ... contract themselves ... with OR WITHOUT a formal capital-C Contract."
- CHECKED (pre-S09 addendum; S01 sheet): Asher has NO formal Contract; his meter is the ACCORDS DEBT LEDGER (every Query and favour owed and paid). His Hook: "Smitten with the Child of the Contract" [Lee]. First True Name rank made him a permanent familiar.
- GM NOTE (the Rule rider): "a spirit BOUND to the examined" -- whether a True-Named familiar with no formal Contract counts as "bound" is ARGUABLE before a Convocation. A formal Contract removes the doubt (and is itself evidence that she ORDERS).

- GM DECISION @@* (setting text, Lee to true -- in the spirit of a real monastic rule's chapter on visiting monks): leaf 15, "OF GUESTS, AND OF BROTHERS OF OTHER HOUSES":
  "A brother of another house, being received, shall keep the CUSTOM OF THE HOUSE that receives him, and shall not trouble it with his own. If he find aught amiss, let him show it to the Prior in humility, and the Prior shall weigh it; but let him not MEND IT WITH HIS OWN HAND."
  (So the Rule itself defers to the house's Customary for guests. Snuffing the Hospitaller's taper is mending with one's own hand.)

S13: "the Hierarch's commission suffices -- see iv"), with a duty of hallowing that overrides custom. Whether that holds -- with Calder under the Visitor's hand -- is an argument for the high days.

- @@* ASHER (flash): "I nodded. He nodded back."
- @@* GODRIC's FINDINGS (from character -- exact, fair, not partisan; facts first, judgment reserved):
  1. "The ALMONER held souls as goods. His own book says so, and his clerk's tally, and his own written confession." (Brask set it down, as he promised S11.)
  2. "The steward's knife, Brother SEVER's chalk, and the clerk TOBIN's hands are proven by more witnesses than I need."
  3. "Brother PELLAM has confessed what he carried and for whom. He told the brother who sits with him one thing more: that there is a BOOK OF NAMES in the HIERARCH's own hand, tied with a RED CORD." A pause; his eyes going slowly along the benches. "That book has not been found. Whoever holds it will bring it to me before the high days end." (He watches the faces as he says it.)
  4. "The room beneath this one I have seen. Whatever was kept there, it was not kept as this Order keeps a watch." (No more than that.)
  5. "Of the HIERARCH I have heard much, from him and of him. I will hear more in this room, in his own voice." (Judgment reserved.)
- GM TRUTH @@* (set now -- it matters this moment; the red-cord book's location was UNDECIDED, "a copy with someone at Convocation" among the candidates): the STOCKY GREY carries CALDER's RED-CORD BOOK inside the breast of his habit (sent out of the house before Calder yielded -- by Sever's hand, to the Hierarch's men at Ganth -- and carried back up with the Ganth party). Whether Brother Superior knows he has it: YES. The young grey: has SEEN it, doesn't know what it is.
- RULING: reading a tell in a crowded room = PERFORMER ("reading crowds like menus") EL8. Presentable/Rule Notes don't apply (not being believed; not citing). Opposition: the STOCKY GREY's composure, LEVEL 3 @@*.
- TIERS (set before the roll): under 3 -- the grey bench stirs; nothing she can pin. = 3 -- at "red cord" the STOCKY GREY's hand goes to the breast of his habit, quick, like a man checking his purse. > 3 -- that, AND the shape under the wool is small and square, book-sized; AND the young grey's eyes went to it.
[Cari Performer: read the room at 'red cord' (EL8)] 8d: 1 1 1 0 0 0 1 0 = 4
[  the stocky grey's composure (Lv3)] 3d: 0 1 0 = 1
- RESULT: 4 vs 1 -> EFFECT 3 = Level 3: a GOOD READ, finest detail held back -- at "red cord" the STOCKY GREY's hand goes to the breast of his habit, quick, like a man checking his purse.
- Offered once: TOKEN (5 -> 4; sets to 8 -> effect 7 > 3: the book-sized shape and the young grey's glance). AWAITING LEE.
- [Lee] TOKEN DECLINED ("we'll wait for now"). Tokens 5. She NOTES the stocky grey's tell; OPENS her SIGHT to see if it gives her anything useful.
- RULES (roles/conjuror.md, The Unseen): the Third Eye -- full Role strength in the Borderlands; clumsy hands; vulnerable to spiritual harm while open. CONJUROR EL9 (Asher not involved -- he's in Calder's hearth). HOOK budget (4) may be added to the RESULT (tapping the Borderlands) -- available.
- Focus: the GREY BENCH, chiefly the STOCKY GREY's breast; the room in passing.
- Opposition: a small object under wool among seventy souls -- faint residue, LEVEL 3 @@*.
- GM TRUTH (set before the roll):
  - Under 3: the room's colours only -- GODRIC a steady grey-white, like a lamp behind horn; BROTHER SUPERIOR narrow and cold-bright (conviction without warmth); the STOCKY GREY hard and brassy (certainty); the YOUNG GREY flickering, fear and wonder in turns. Nothing on the book.
  - = 3: AND at the stocky grey's breast a small DEAD SPOT -- a patch of COLD under the wool, like a stone lifted out of a winter stream: the taste of the BORROWED COLD she knows from CALDER's palms.
  - > 3: AND the finest detail -- it tastes exactly like the HIERARCH's LEAD TOKEN in Ruddock's kerchief (the same hand, the same leash); and BROTHER SUPERIOR's colour LEANS toward that spot whenever the Visitor speaks of the book (he knows it's there).
[Cari Sight (Conjuror EL9): the grey bench] 9d: 0 1 1 0 1 1 1 1 1 = 7
[  faint residue under wool (Lv3)] 3d: 1 0 1 = 2
- RESULT: 7 vs 2 -> EFFECT 5 > 3: COMPLETE -- all three tiers (the colours; the COLD dead spot at the stocky grey's breast, Calder's borrowed cold; it tastes like the HIERARCH's LEAD TOKEN; BROTHER SUPERIOR's colour leans toward it at the Visitor's words). Hook budget unused (4).
- WKW: CARI (by Sight) -- the stocky grey carries something of CALDER's, cold, at his breast, matching the lead token; Brother Superior knows. (She does not SEE that it is a book -- only that it is small, cold, his, and hidden there.)
- [Lee] "It's enough for now." Lets the Third Eye close; EYES on GODRIC, ATTENTION on the STOCKY GREY and BROTHER SUPERIOR. Ready to intervene, as with "the ALMONER's SEAL that sits in the lavabo now" [Lee's name for Calder's drowned packet under the dark wax]; for now lets the process do what it was meant to do.
- (Sight closed -- no lingering vulnerability.)
- @@* GODRIC (from character -- judgments within his own authority, brief; the Reading of Names kept to its rule):
  - "BRASK is put from the office of Almoner and from this house. He goes under guard to a house of penance at my pleasure." @@ which house: UNDECIDED. "SEVER likewise. The STEWARD to the Count's justice for the knife" @@* (secular arm; whose: UNDECIDED). "The clerk TOBIN is a novice; his vows were never taken. He goes home to his father with what he came with, and no more." "Brother PELLAM told the truth when it cost him. He will choose his penance, and I will approve it."
  - Then: "The Reading of Names." A beat. "No name may be laid that was not written in this house's book a full season since. SACRIST -- the book."

- @@* ASHER (flash): "He's walking. By himself. I'm coming back." (Back to her bracer as Calder leaves his room.)
- @@* CALDER's ENTRANCE (situation, from character -- a sincere man corrupted; yielded S11; days since; "not afraid any more"): the door; the Visitor's two men; CALDER between them, walking unaided -- gaunt, shaved fresh, in BLACK, gloved to the wrist, the gloves new. - @@* GODRIC: "Brother Voss Calder. You hold a commission of the Order, sealed in this house. Two names were written at your asking a season since. Will you lay them, in your own voice?"

  "IVRO TAMSETT, Precentor. Under the Rule, the seventeenth leaf." A breath. "He holds a soul as goods. He BOUGHT her, at nine years old, from her father, for silver." (He does not know the sum.) "Ask him if it is not so."

- @@* BROTHER SUPERIOR (rising; from character -- narrow, cold-bright, exact, never raises his voice; his first public words): "Father Visitor." A bow, correct. - [Lee] Walks forward to stand beside IVRO as she speaks. QUOTES the page, paragraph and line of the Rule where the exception is made for WARDS (leaf 17, "Of Goods and Holdings": "...save in wardship, as a father his child, unto their age"). "This man is the only FATHER I acknowledge, and has called me DAUGHTER in front of witnesses, including the Prior. Opens her arms to show her Colours; turns to the benches behind and back; smiles warmly at Ivro. [  the charge as laid and argued (Lv4)] 4d: 0 0 0 0 = 0 (EXTREME FAILURE)
- RESULT: 10 vs 0 -> EFFECT 10 > 4: COMPLETE. AND the opposition rolled an EXTREME FAILURE (4 for 4 blank) -> a significant SIDE EFFECT against the charge's side (Lee S15 rule). Hook budget unused.
- @@* GODRIC's RULING (from character): "The Rule makes the exception, and the Initiate has read it to us by the line." A beat. "It leaves the AGE to the house. - @@* BROTHER SUPERIOR's EVIDENCE (from character -- exact, cold, thorough; from his tablet and what the grey ones could have; GM decisions @@*):
  1. "Her robes came down to the laundry smelling of SULPHUR before midsummer. The laundress said so to the Hierarch's clerks, and to you, Father Visitor." (Sabeth -- on record.)
  2. "In the Prior's own room, before a recording clerk and a prisoner, the brazier ROSE of itself and there was brimstone." (Odo's book -- on record.)
  3. "Last night, at the Office in the guest chapel, she breathed brimstone at three brothers of the Order at their prayers. At her word, with no hand near it, a spirit lit a taper we had put out. I watched it. It LEANED to her." (His tablet.)
  4. "This morning at first light, in the HIERARCH's own hearth, a fire that is HERS -- set there by her hand, as the house knows -- went up the chimney a hand high. On the high day. Brothers saw it from the garth." (The dawn side effect -- now evidence: she keeps a fire, and eyes, in the accuser's own room.)
  5. "And I call Brother EDWY of the Shrike Hollow circuit, who sat with her at supper." 
- @@* EDWY (from character -- white to the lips, honest to the bone; stands): "Brother?" BROTHER SUPERIOR: "Did you smell sulphur on the Initiate last evening?" EDWY (a long breath; he looks at her once, miserable, then answers the man who asked): "Yes, Brother." A beat -- and then, because he cannot leave a truth half-told: "In the kitchen. - @@* EDWY (from character -- honest; steadier with each true thing):
  1. "Yes, Sister. He asked down our bench which of the house brothers were IN VOWS. And which of them talk to you. And which house I was professed of. He wrote it all down."
  2. "You didn't break anything." A pause; scrupulous. "You scowled. You said it would be easier to respect them if they didn't use such--" he swallows "--'prissy and underhanded methods.'"   3. "Mes Tilda said, 'There's the Sister's weather.' The little scullion laughed. Mes Mags didn't turn round." A beat. "And then YOU laughed -- loud -- and it went away." - @@* BROTHER SUPERIOR (from character): "One thing more, Father Visitor." To the stool: "The HIERARCH's hands."
- @@* CALDER (from character -- sincere, careful; believes his hands are the price of holding the Watch): takes off the LEFT glove, slowly, finger by finger. He lays it bare on his knee -- and FROST blooms on the black wool under it, a white hand-shaped bloom, spreading a finger's width before it stops. The front benches lean away. "Her fire did this. At the ford, when I put down what the Pit had raised from a dead brother." (His version -- the S11 high-table story; Godric heard it Day 20.) He does not put the glove back on.

- @@* GODRIC: "Is that all, brother?" BROTHER SUPERIOR: "That is all." GODRIC (to her): "Then answer, Initiate."

- @@* CALDER (when she meets his gaze): meets it; doesn't speak; the bare hand stays on his knee; the frost has stopped spreading.

- NO ROLL (testimony; she invited the attention -- no Hook trigger; holding calm is her choice, not a contest).
- (THE TELLING, as before -- clerk-plain, nothing hidden): Tanner's Ford, night; Korrin's train, Kade and Hollis, the drovers, Jem sick in the wagon; the Sight-find in the reeds and the brimstone-kinship ("it knew me by the same mark the Hierarch has since spoken of"); her light over the water; what the camp saw by plain eye; AUBRIC's words -- "...Cari." / "Don't look at me, little sister." / "He walks me to the sea, to the woman who shamed him"; who heard them (Kade); the leash by Sight running NORTH; her rebuke down it; the line burning back into the holder's hands; that the holder TASTED what is in her; the exorcism; the burial at Saltwind; the keys with the orange ribbon; and, briefly, that she took pleasure in his pain, and has confessed it to her gods since.
- @@* (the room, through it): at "Don't look at me, little sister," HANNET's hand goes to her mouth; WYSTAN bows his head; at "the line burning back into the holder's hands," every eye in the room goes, without anyone deciding to, to the BARE HAND on CALDER's knee. Calder does not move it. Her voice holds. Her eyes are wet at the end and she does not let it be more than that.
- @@* KADE (from character -- the Finder; plain, hard, honest; reported by mouth to the Prior; his written reports went to Brask): "I saw a dead man in the reeds by her light. Grey. Filmed eyes. "Then she put him to rest, and we buried him at Saltwind. I carried him." And then the GM DECISION @@* (from Kade's position -- the Almoner's Finder, in Brask's office; set now, it matters): "At midsummer the Hierarch asked me to watch her for SULPHUR. That's the word he used. He never said DEMON." He looks at the stool. "The first time I heard him say 'demon-blooded' was to the Almoner, the night we came back up from the coast. His hands were in new bandages." (Timeline check: the ford ~Day 1; back at the Mountain Day 5 (S10); Calder yielded Day 6 -- consistent.)"
- @@* GODRIC (very still): "After." Not a question. ODO writes. (The Visitor's Day 21 test: if Calder named her demon-touched only AFTER the ford, the knowledge came down his own leash.)
- @@* CALDER: closes his bare hand on his knee. The frost cracks.
- @@* GODRIC (to her): "You've told it. The Finder's told it. Now -- your answer to the charge, Initiate. Do you ORDER what plagues you, or are you ORDERED?"
- WKW: the WHOLE CONVOCATION -- her tale of the ford; KADE's testimony (Aubric spoke her name "like Aubric"; she put him to rest; Calder asked for SULPHUR at midsummer, said "DEMON-BLOODED" first the night they came back from the coast, hands newly bandaged); her "not yet."
- [Lee] CARI's ANSWER (the Examination): the Visitor himself saw her turn away from the rising power of her blood that wanted to burn Pellam in the Prior's office -- "Sometimes it is not EASY, and there are tears." "If a bit of brimstone is all the evidence you have, I can do that for you on command. It means I have touched that power, and I do so when service to my god and the Tenets of our Order demand it." Points at CALDER: "THAT is not a thing I can do." To the Assembly: "Is there a man or woman in this room who can say they have seen me once produce ICE? No mistake -- we'll address that, but this is a red herring. The Examination is whether I Order, or am Ordered." Were she not in command of herself: seeing these men snuff Aubric's votive taper against House custom would have had her burn all three to ash, rather than calling a pure flame to keep it lit; she'd have flown back to avenge Aubric on Calder before laying him to rest; wouldn't have taken the time to speak with the folk of Saltwind and learn of the CORVANE girl. To the grey bench: wouldn't have remained calm "every time the brother there touches his breast when the Father Visitor mentions the Hierarch's little book with the red cords." Would have burned Calder, Sever and Tobin -- but killed NONE of them when she had the chance, even the one who tried to stab her in the back, even in clear self-defence and the defence of the House, which would have left her today without this accuser. "I knew this day was coming... and yet here we stand."

  - The FELLS' graveside verse (S12; Teague): the hill-folk sing at the coffin's lid -- "Take not the Cold, though it give thee its hand... and the least of the house take up the brand" -- words that are NOT in any copy of the Charter. That's what sent her to the ORIGINAL.
  - The ORIGINAL in its cedar box (three vellum leaves); the end of the first leaf deliberately SCRAPED, long ago; read again by RAKING LIGHT at Merrit's desk, the PRECENTOR witnessing: the two lost lines (quoted whole). And "Lies," capitalised, where every copy says "sleeps."
  - Every copy in the house descends from ONE exemplar, made "fair and whole" under PRIOR CUTHRED; Cuthred's rule that copyists work from the exemplar and NEVER the original "to spare the vellum" -- so for generations no one read the end of the line.
  - The CUSTOMARY's oldest hand provided READERS OF THE CHARTER, chosen by the brothers, to read the Founder's leaves ALOUD, whole, once a year "that none may say he was not told" -- "laid aside, the leaves being spoilt," in CUTHRED's time.
  - The READING OF NAMES chapter -- the procedure the Convocation used this morning, and its clause for "one holding the Order's commission" -- is in CUTHRED's HAND, on leaves SEWN IN later.
  - (Withheld, per the Prior: the Founder's letters, the cipher, the name; Cuthred's wax by Sight.)

Been growing moss for twenty years waiting for somebody to want it." A beat. "Get RUDDOCK to carry it. He likes carrying things that don't talk back."
- GM DECISION @@* (setting; Lee to true): the BROKEN CLOISTER COLUMN-DRUM -- dressed grey stone, knee-high, a cracked top face, moss in the fluting; in the masons' corner by the woodshed. With the mortar on it, a fire-safe shrine by the Precentor's door.

- @@* THE MASONS' CORNER (situation): behind the woodshed, out of the wind -- a lean-to roof, stacked offcuts, a rusting crowbar, a pile of roof-slates; and the COLUMN DRUM, half sunk in dead nettles: dressed grey stone, a little over knee-high, as broad as a cartwheel's hub is long @@; the top face cracked across where it broke from its fellows, moss green in the fluting and a cushion of it on top. Under the moss on the side, a MASON's MARK -- a small chiselled arrow with a bar through it (the old cloister mason's sign @@*). HEAVY: more than two men's lift -- a barrow, rollers, or Ruddock and a friend.
- @@* (a small thing): a wren goes out of the nettles at her feet, scolding, and sits on the woodshed ridge to watch.
- [Lee] OOC/plan: the drum's a big hunk of stone to get up the library stairs -- big enough to kill someone if something went badly. Is there a WINDOW she could STONE SHAPE it through to get it into place safely? (It'd probably clean it, too.)
- RULES READ (magick/spells.md, Stone Shape): "Manipulates EXISTING stone within range. Can create spikes for attacks, barriers for defense, or utility effects like handholds or concealment." (Bridge example: coaxed a bridge across, let it crumble.) -> it SHAPES stone where it is; it does NOT lift, levitate or carry. SHEET: Stone Shape is UNBOUND since S14 Day 17 -> full RITUAL at EL7 (Spellweaver unbound).
- GM ANSWER (within the text): it can't float the drum through a window. It CAN: (a) HOLLOW the drum's core -- a cup for the mortar to sit IN, and a great deal lighter (more than half its weight gone) -- so three men can carry it up the stair; (b) raise HANDHOLDS on its flanks for a safe grip; (c) take the moss and grime off as it reshapes the outer skin -- clean. (d) Reshaping the LIBRARY WALL or a window to pass it would be altering the house's fabric -- Ivro's library, the Prior's house -- not a thing done without leave, and a narrow lancet is a lancet for a reason. (Lee's call.)
- [Lee] DESIGN READ: Stone Shape SHAPES -- so creatively, a PSEUDOPOD reaches up from the ground through an open sill; shaping the mass onto that foot PUMPS the stone up through the window like water through a pipe that is itself the stone; once there, the same to move it into place; then release -- "the stone reverts to its original shape if it can. Right?"
- RULES READ: magick/spells.md (Improvise: each useful Trait +1 Difficulty, each limiting Trait -1, min 0; a Difficult spell needs a free-action HOLD roll vs its modified Difficulty); traits.md (PERSISTENT -- continues without concentration; GUIDABLE -- keep directing it while line of sight holds); Stone Shape text ("manipulates existing stone"; the BRIDGE: "coaxed a new bridge across; when the last of us stepped off, he let it CRUMBLE TO DUST").
- GM RULING @@* (Lee to true): YES -- the pseudopod pump is within "manipulates existing stone" (the bridge moved mass across a gap). Mechanics: UNBOUND -> full ritual at EL7. Improvised PERSISTENT +1, GUIDABLE +1 (line of sight: she watches from the library window), CONSTRAINED -1 (only the drum's own stone -- it touches no stone of the house) -> HOLD vs DL1. Then the WORKING at EL7 vs the scale of the change (a column drum, two storeys up, through a sill) LEVEL 3 @@*.
- GM CAVEAT (from the spell's own text): RELEASE is NOT REVERT -- the bridge-maker "let it crumble to dust." Shaped stone doesn't go home by itself; let go mid-shape and she may get grit on Ivro's floor. So: she SHAPES IT BACK to its final form (drum, hollowed cup, handholds -- whatever she wants it to BE) WHILE the spell is held, THEN releases. The rolling across the court to below the window is ordinary work (rollers, Ruddock).
- [Lee] CORRECTIONS (design): CONSTRAINED here is +1, not -1 -- it's a feature requiring MORE effort (precision: only the drum's stone, touching none of the house), not one making it easier. GUIDABLE not needed -- directing is INHERENT in shaping (no argument either way). PERSISTENT isn't strictly needed, but SAFER to have (she'll likely break line of sight). The BRIDGE crumbles because it CAN'T go back -- a limiting factor on the spell's power; sudden SPIKES used as defence are much the same. -> RELEASE REVERTS the stone to its original shape IF IT CAN.
- RULING (Lee's): Stone Shape, UNBOUND -> full ritual EL7. Improvised PERSISTENT +1, CONSTRAINED (as precision) +1 -> HOLD vs DL2. The WORKING EL7 vs the scale of the change, LEVEL 3 @@*. On release the drum returns to its own shape (whatever was taken from it) where it then stands -- so any NEW shape she wants it to keep (a hollow cup, handholds) is a separate matter @@ (Lee to say how she fixes a new shape, if she wants one).
- [Lee] RULING: fixing a NEW PERMANENT shape is an ENCHANTMENT -- an ITEM-CREATION roll with a PERSISTENT Trait at least; definitely nontrivial. (-> gap log.)
- [Lee] LEAVES the drum where it is for now: she'd want to talk first to whoever might have been using it as a SEAT -- and doesn't want to walk "a giant stone inchworm across the garth today."
- @@* (situation, a small thing she notices as she turns to go): the moss on the drum's top has a FLATTENED patch the size of a man's seat, and in the nettles below, three APPLE CORES of different ages and a whittled stick. Somebody sits here, out of the wind, regularly. Who: UNDECIDED.

  - The ORDER in the north = this ONE HOUSE on Mount Cunae + its CIRCUITS (fits "the Itinerant Saint": the circuits ARE the Order's presence in the villages) + wayside shrines/chapels (Saltwind's chapel yard; Becklin's stone at Crossroads).   - READERS: ONE SET for the house and its circuits is enough -- recommend THREE (Cari's draft: "preferably three, to regulate each other"), with at least ONE a circuit brother so the villages' side is in the room. (Lee's call.)

- CHECKED: NOT on record. On record only: leaf 11 "Of Charges" -- a charge in one's own voice, not "by letter, clerk or another's mouth"; and (C) "Who lays the charge of the black art and cannot prove it before the Convocation shall bear what the accused would have borne." Godric (Day 21): "Necromancy" is "a word for a court of the Order." The Order serves the god of Demons ("learn to Order what Plagues us"; the Toll: "Rites to give Demons Limits") -- so demon-work as such cannot be the black art.
- GM DRAFT @@* (Lee to true; leaf 11, beside the false-charge clause -- Cari read leaf 11 on Day 23, so NO ROLL): the Rule names DEEDS, not schools; the word "necromancy" is not in it (that is the commentators' word, and carries Tahanos's weight in Saltwind -- why Godric flinched).
    "That art is black which TAKES what is not GIVEN: the LIFE of the living, the REST of the dead, or the WILL of any spirit, save by the Accords."
  Lines it draws: asking a willing dead soul = given (lawful); calling one up unwilling, or working a corpse = takes rest (black); leeching life = black (the Cold's craft; Calder's seeds); binding a spirit outside a freely struck Accord = takes will (black). Formal Contracts (Asher) are given.
  Open edges for Lee: (1) does a Rebuke or exorcism "take will"? (draft: a threshold refused is not will taken -- you have a right to your own door); (2) the dead's consent -- who speaks for a soul that cannot answer?; (3) Ivro's purchase sits under leaf 17 (souls as goods), not here.
- [Lee] on the draft -- "almost perfect." Rikitak is by definition the rulebreaker (hard to write good rules for). Canon treats spirits, even of the dead, as RESOURCES to be exploited -- a dark starting point, hard to vilify; the ACCORDS protect both sides, and may be enough: any Working that BINDS A FREE WILL outside the Accords. Taking a LIFE is less the concern (a caravan guard stabbing a bandit; a fireball is not so different -- but people are far more suspicious of the fireball, and rightly suspect a deal with a "demon," often a fire spirit here). "ORDERED is probably the root of it. 'Wear thee as a Glove...'" Crimes are crimes, knife or spell; the spell only draws more scrutiny.
- GM DRAFT 2 @@* (Lee to true; leaf 11): "That art is black which binds a free will outside the Accords: to wear another, or to Accept being worn."
  Notes: life-taking drops out -- a killing is judged as a killing, by ordinary law, whatever the tool. "Accept" capitalized for the Charter Clause ("Accept not the Cold ... and wear thee as a Glove"). Being worn UNWILLING is a PLAGUE, examined under leaf 6 (Orders vs is Ordered) -- treated, not punished; ACCEPTING it is the black art (Calder). The extra SCRUTINY a spell draws is the reason leaf 6 exists. Aubric's corpse still counts: his spirit was LEASHED (S11), a will bound.

## DAY 26 -- afternoon into night: the ROAD to CROSSROADS
- RULING: NO ROLL for the frost -- they LEAD the beasts down the iced stretches (Jem's drover sense: "You don't ride a mule on ice, Sister. You walk her."). Sensible care, no pressure.

- @@* OLD MAN: OSWIN TANNE @@* (name new), seventies, a retired COOPER [GM retcon Day 27: was "thatcher" -- clashes with S13: Crossroads LOST its thatcher (CORRAN, Aldy's husband) last winter]; lives alone in the end cottage on the Ford lane.

CAUSE: his chimney draws badly -- a JACKDAW NEST in the flue since autumn -- and the cottage fills with smoke every morning when he lights up. Worse indoors, better out. Hiddenness 3 (the cause). TIERS: under 3 = a lingering chest cold, wet at the bases -> steam, honey, keep warm; EQUAL = + it's SMOKE (smell in his coat, soot in the creases of his knuckles, worse at home in the mornings) -- but not why; OVER = + the cause itself: the birds in the flue (he'll mention "the daws in the thatch" in passing; she catches it).
- ROLL: Cari HEALER EL9 (no Boost) vs 3. The easy face needs no roll -- he coughs, she listens, ear to his back.
[Cari Healer: Oswin's cough (EL9 vs 3)] 9d: 1 1 1 1 1 1 1 0 1 = 8
- RESULT: 8 vs 3 -> OVER, effect 5: the WHOLE truth -- the cough, the wet bases, the smoke, and the JACKDAWS in the flue. (Not an Extreme: one die came up 0.)

- GM TRUTH (set before the roll; @@*): no fever; no illness. A DRIED PEA, pushed deep into the LEFT ear canal (he's been playing in his mam's pea-crock). Hiddenness 3 (deep, behind wax; a squirming toddler). TIERS: under 3 = an earache, a little red -> warm oil and wait (WRONG -- oil would swell the pea); EQUAL = SOMETHING is in the canal, she can't tell what -- no oil; OVER = a DRIED PEA, and how it comes out (tip him, coax, a hooked bodkin or a blunt loop, never push; NO oil, no water -- it swells).

- RESULT: 5 vs 3 -> OVER, effect 2: the whole truth -- a DRIED PEA deep in the left canal; NO OIL; it comes out by tipping and coaxing, never pushing.

- [Lee] To ELSPET: she was SMART not to do it without asking. Shows her the pea. Solemnly: "There's an old bit of wisdom: forbid a child to shove a pea up his nose, and he will shove a pea up his nose." Smirks. "The trick is that we have to get behind it, but we don't want to go too deep and ruin his hearing -- also, that would really hurt." Asks (OOC): may she have a small LOOP (like a surgical needle-threader), or Token a well-stocked Healer's kit?
- RULING (gear = narrative permission; what-gear-does.md): NO TOKEN. A small double-ended bronze EAR-SCOOP with a wire LOOP at the other end is ordinary trade kit for a Healer -- as common as a comb -- so it is in her satchel. A full SURGEON's ROLL (saws, lancets, cautery) is NOT ordinary for an Initiate on the road -- that would be Hannet's, or a Token. -> gap log.
- GM TRUTH (set before the roll; @@*): the pea is deep but not against the drum; Hob will squirm. Opposition 3. ELSPET holding him on her lap, cheek to her breast, = +1 simple Boost (anyone may hold a child; Lee ruling S15 l.133). TIERS: under 3 = not out -- it slips a little deeper, Hob howls; try again after he calms (no harm); EQUAL = OUT, with a small scrape in the canal (a spot of blood, sore a day or two; hearing fine); OVER = out clean. An Extreme failure = the drum is nicked (a Harm to Hob).
- ROLL: Cari HEALER EL9 + 1 (Elspet holds) = 10d vs 3.
[Cari Healer: the pea out (EL9+1 vs 3)] 10d: 0 1 0 0 1 0 0 1 0 0 = 3
- RESULT: 3 vs 3 -> EQUAL: the pea is OUT, with a small SCRAPE in the canal -- a spot of blood on the loop; sore a day or two; hearing fine. TOKEN OFFERED (once): Tokens 3 -- spend one to set the roll to its EL (10) = out clean.

The graze is struck.

- GM DECISION @@* (the PRIOR's LETTER to the Council -- contents, broad strokes, from character: dry, exact, keeps her out of the line of fire; written Day 25 after the Convocation, round the binding on his arm; sealed):
  1. THANKS for the Council's letter -- read at CHAPTER and again at CONVOCATION, "to the last small line."
  2. NOTICE: the Hierarch VOSS CALDER is put out of the Order and goes to Ganth under the Visitor's guard to answer to the Council of the Free City for the children of the region. (Plain; no ornament.)
  3. THE WALKING-IN: approved -- "Let them. A house that fears to be watched has no business being trusted."
  4. A CIRCUIT: GRANTED. A priest of the house will be NAMED to the round of Crossroads before the spring thaw (WHO: still UNDECIDED). Until then the house's order stands: every priest of the house who passes stops a day, keeps the office at the shrine, and asks at the pump what is wanted.
  5. THE OFFER of bed and board: accepted with thanks. THE BOWLS: the circuit takes NOTHING from them; what is given at the shrine stays in the town ("it has found a better use than the road").
  6. THE LAST LINE: "The Council's last line was also heard at Convocation." Nothing more.
  7. A POSTSCRIPT, smaller, in the same awkward hand: the Sister's bed and board at the inn are to be charged to the house, "and Mes Berra is to be told so firmly, though I do not expect her to listen."

At the postscript, a short, shocked bark of a laugh he plainly didn't mean to let out. Then he folds it, very carefully, along its own creases. "...A circuit." Rough. "Proper." A breath. "He says you're to bring him our answer. In my words." A look at her. "I'll have it by the dusk lamp. I'll want to show Bram and Garrow first." A beat; then, because he is an honest man: "And you'll not read that one either, I expect." Behind him, WAT's MOTHER, at the inner door, avid as ever.

## DAY 27 -- late morning: GARROW HASK's SMITHY
- Present: Cari; GARROW HASK; OLD BELLOWS (the forge); JEM (trailing, to watch). (Same morning round: no Token refresh. Tokens 2. Hook budget 4.)

BELLOWS -- TEMPERED (black jet coal, live ember sealed inside, NEEDS NO FUEL; S09/S06 the sea); fire-speech heard by Cari only, others see the fire move; smug, proud, loves an audience; "Seasoned, every day"; called her RUDE for riding out without stopping in.

Then, dangerously sweet: "Bring it here, girl. Closer. I'll show you what green does."

Introduces them: "Master Garrow(s), Old Bellows, this is Jem, my friend and apprentice. Jem, master Garrow is obviously the blacksmith, and Old Bellows is obviously the spirit of the forge -- maybe not as old as Old Soot in the kitchens, but a lot more talkative and opinionated." Pulls up a chair. Jem hasn't heard all of this yet either; she'll tell every bit as much as she's ALLOWED to (the gag: the NAME the Founder's key gave and "of Tahanos" stay unsaid).
- RULE (register sec 17; S15 l.473 precedent): hag-stone = a narrow Second Sight glimpse at BASE LEVEL. JEM Level 1 -> 1d. OPPOSITION: none -- BELLOWS WANTS to be seen (he's a show-off; from character). 
- GM TRUTH (set before the roll; @@*): through the stone, the forge holds BELLOWS -- the jet coal is his HEART, black glass with a red spark sealed in it; around it a squat, broad-chested shape of red-gold fire, puffed cheeks like a smith's bellows, arms folded, PREENING; the salt-glaze of the sea glitters on him like frost on iron. TIERS: 1 = Jem SEES him (and Bellows poses); 0 = only the coal, glassy and bright -- nothing looks back.
[Jem through the hag-stone at Bellows (base Lv1, no opp)] 1d: 0 = 0
- RESULT: 0 -> Jem sees ONLY the coal: black glass, a red spark, bright -- nothing looks back. TOKEN OFFERED (once; anyone may spend on any roll, an NPC's included): Tokens 2 -> spend one to set Jem's roll to its EL (1) = he sees Bellows.
- @@* BELLOWS (fire-speech, offended, to Cari only): "He's looking AT me through a ROCK and he can't SEE me? I'm STANDING here." A puff of sparks that is pure vanity. "Tell him to look HARDER."

- (Pending Lee: the Token; then the TELLING.)
- [Lee] "Give me a moment," grinning. Explains to JEM that sometimes we need to give ourselves a little help; tells him to REMEMBER what it was like to see ASHER (and Old Soot) -- teaches him to make his own Boost. Cari rolls a Boost FOR him while she coaches. (No Token.)
- RULES READ: actions-and-timing.md (Boost Actions: "Success gives you a temporary bonus that makes a following action more likely to succeed"); assist-rule.md (help from something with its own capabilities is capped by the receiver's rank in the relevant Role; no rank = no Boost).
- RULING (GM, for Lee to true): Cari's COACHING = a Boost action, CONJUROR EL9 (her craft of Sight), opposition 0 (nothing resists; Jem wants it). The hag-stone glimpse is a BASE-LEVEL act (no Role needed), so -- by analogy with Lee's S15 l.133 ruling (things anyone can do work at base level) -- the Boost Jem can USE is capped at his BASE LEVEL (1), not at a Role rank he lacks. Then JEM looks again: base 1 + Boost = 2d, no opposition. -> gap log.
[Cari Conjuror: coach Jem's Sight (EL9 vs 0; Boost cap 1)] 9d: 1 0 0 0 1 1 0 0 0 = 3
- RESULT: 3 vs 0 -> EFFECT 3 -> BOOST capped at Jem's base Level: +1. JEM's second look: 1 + 1 = 2d, no opposition (same truth and tiers as above; any success = he SEES him).
[Jem through the hag-stone again (base 1 + Boost 1, no opp)] 2d: 1 0 = 1
- RESULT: 1 (of 2d; within EL2) vs 0 -> SUCCESS: JEM SEES BELLOWS -- the squat red-gold shape round the black-glass heart, cheeks puffed, arms folded; the salt-glaze glittering; and BELLOWS, seeing himself seen, POSES (chest out, one fist on hip).
- WKW: JEM -- he has seen a second spirit (after Old Soot and Asher); he did it himself, on her coaching. BELLOWS -- the apprentice can see him. GARROW -- sees the boy see something.
- [Lee] To JEM: "Now show Mer Garrow. Let him use the stone, help him see it. He probably never has." Gives them a little time.
- GM DECISION @@* (needed now; grep-clean): GARROW HASK base LEVEL 3 (a master smith in his fifties). Hag-stone glimpse at base level -> 3d.
- RULING (as just set, gap log): JEM coaches -- a base-level act for him (he's just done it; no Role) -> 1d vs 0; the Boost Garrow can use is capped at Garrow's base Level (3), so up to +1 here. Then GARROW looks: 3d + Boost, no opposition (Bellows wants to be seen; Garrow believes since S13). "A little time" (no pressure) -> no penalty, no hurry.
- GM TRUTH (set before the rolls; @@*): any success = GARROW SEES BELLOWS -- and Bellows, for once, does NOT pose: he goes still and small and looks back at the man who has fed him thirty years, like a dog caught on the bed. EXTREME SUCCESS (all 1s on 3+ dice) = he also HEARS one word of fire-speech unaided ("Hask."). Failure = only the coal. Extreme failure = only the coal, and a stinging eye from staring into the forge (no Harm).
[Jem coaches Garrow (base 1 vs 0; cap Garrow base 3)] 1d: 1 = 1
- RESULT: 1 vs 0 -> BOOST +1. GARROW looks: 3 + 1 = 4d, no opposition.
[Garrow through the hag-stone (base 3 + Boost 1, no opp)] 4d: 0 1 0 1 = 2
- RESULT: 2 (within EL4) vs 0 -> SUCCESS: GARROW SEES BELLOWS -- and Bellows goes still and small and looks back. (No Extreme.)
- @@* BELLOWS (fire-speech, to Cari only, very quiet -- from character; the S13 Hild beat in reverse): "...He's LOOKING at me." A long pause; the coal barely glowing. "Thirty years. BELLOWS keeps time as he did with the sea -- low at the dark parts, a hiss at Calder's cold hands, and at the Charter read whole, a slow, deep brightening like a held breath let go.

- GM TRUTH (set before the roll; @@*): Bram's RIGHT shoulder is strained from the HOPPER LIFT -- he takes a sack up the ladder on the right shoulder, then at the top shoves it UP and over the hopper's lip with a TWIST, one-handed, because his left holds the ladder. WHY he does it alone: BENNO used to stand on the hopper-step and STEADY the sack's mouth for him -- since the leg (last spring), Bram's done it alone and said nothing. TIERS (information roll: equal = a good read, finest detail held back): under 2 = he favours the right arm, nothing more; EQUAL = it's the HOPPER LIFT -- the one-handed overhead shove with a twist; OVER = + the WHY: a two-man job done by one, the boy's old place on the hopper-step empty (a little worn step-block, swept, unused).
- ROLL: Cari HEALER EL9 vs 2 (by eye). (Bram growing self-conscious is colour, not opposition.)
[Cari Healer: watch Bram's shoulder (EL9 vs 2)] 9d: 0 1 1 1 0 0 0 0 0 = 3
- RESULT: 3 vs 2 -> OVER, effect 1: the WHOLE truth -- the right shoulder; the one-handed overhead twist at the hopper; and the WHY: Benno's old place on the hopper-step (a worn step-block, swept, unused) -- a two-man job done by one since the leg.
- @@* BRAM (from character -- heavy, slow, measured; self-conscious under the look): the second sack goes in with a grunt he tries to swallow. Comes down the ladder. Stands there floury, rolling the shoulder once before he can stop himself. "...Sister." A pause, measured. "You'll be wanting something." (He knows when he's being looked at.)
- GM ANSWER (Lee asked what she could SENSIBLY suggest; her training and a miller's world -- shape only, Lee's to say): (1) the job is a TWO-MAN job -- someone on the hopper-step again to take the sack's mouth (BENNO could stand that: still, on a step, both hands -- no running, no stairs under load); (2) a ROPE over the beam to the hopper, a pulley-basket or a sack-hook (cf. the laundry hoist, S15) -- or, in time, a SACK HOIST off the wheel itself (real mills had them; Garrow and a carpenter's work @@); (3) HALF-sacks till it mends; (4) the shoulder itself: rest from overhead work, warmth, and LINIMENT -- the very crock she brought for Aldy -- "and let Ebba rub it." Mechanically: no Harm on Bram's sheet @@ (a strain, as Ivro's ribs: Hindrance if it matters; it doesn't today).
- WKW: CARI -- Bram's shoulder and its cause, including Benno's empty step.
